/bin/bash -lc "sed -n '1,210p' reference-interpreter/isa_check.py" in /home/tobias/Projects/bennett-workspace/bennett-isa-spec
 succeeded in 0ms:
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Pre-flight check for spec/Bennett-ISA-Spec.md (seed of the reference interpreter): the example objects must parse,
their hashes must follow the spec's own envelope rule, and their stated
semantics must hold on a reference interpreter. Run before every council
round (council.py prepare --precheck "python3 tools/isa_check.py").

What it implements (from the spec text; version-pinned by the header line):
  - §16  canonical text: header, section order, data lines, function blocks
  - §9.2 semantic envelope + SCC hashing (Tarjan over @symbol references)
  - §14.1 reference interpreter for the lifted subset the examples use:
         lift.const.iN lift.add lift.sub lift.icmp.* lift.zext.iM lift.mov
         lift.addr lift.boundscheck lift.load.iN lift.call lift.ret
         lift.retval qjump qbranch, with activation records, alloctab
         pre-entries, budgets (steps/depth) and fault codes
Exit 1 on any mismatch; prints what differed.
"""
import hashlib
import json
import re
import sys
from pathlib import Path

SPEC = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent / "spec" / "Bennett-ISA-Spec.md"
SECTION_ORDER = ["symbols", "imports", "functions", "data", "devices", "layout",
                 "schedule", "certificate", "coverage"]
FAULT = {"NONE": 0, "STEPS": 1, "HEAP": 2, "DEPTH": 3, "TRAP": 4, "DIV0": 5,
         "SDIV": 6, "MEMFAULT": 7, "EFFECT": 8, "CALLEE": 9}


def jcs(o):
    return json.dumps(o, sort_keys=True, separators=(",", ":"), ensure_ascii=False)


def sha(b):
    return hashlib.sha256(b).hexdigest()


# ----------------------------------------------------------------- parsing

def extract_examples(text):
    """Code blocks that start with 'bennett-object' are example objects."""
    blocks = re.findall(r"```\n(bennett-object[^\n]*\n.*?)```", text, re.S)
    return [b.rstrip("\n") + "\n" for b in blocks]


def parse_object(obj):
    lines = obj.rstrip("\n").split("\n")
    assert lines[0].startswith("bennett-object "), "missing header"
    hdr = dict(kv.split("=") for kv in lines[0].split(" ")[1:])
    sections, cur, order = {}, None, []
    i = 1
    while i < len(lines):
        ln = lines[i]
        if ln == "":
            assert i + 1 < len(lines) and lines[i + 1].startswith("section "), \
                f"blank line not followed by section at line {i}"
            i += 1
            continue
        if ln.startswith("section "):
            cur = ln.split(" ", 1)[1]
            assert cur in SECTION_ORDER, f"unknown section {cur}"
            assert cur not in sections, f"duplicate section {cur}"
            order.append(cur)
            sections[cur] = []
            i += 1
            continue
        assert cur is not None, "content before any section"
        sections[cur].append(ln)
        i += 1
    assert order == [s for s in SECTION_ORDER if s in order], f"section order {order}"
    for s in order:
        if s not in ("functions", "data"):
            assert len(sections[s]) == 1, f"section {s} must be one JSON line"
            sections[s] = json.loads(sections[s][0])
            raw = lines[[k for k, l in enumerate(lines) if l == "section " + s][0] + 1]
            assert jcs(sections[s]) == raw, f"section {s} is not JCS-canonical"
    return hdr, sections


def parse_functions(lines):
    """Split the functions section into functions; each: header, regs, blocks."""
    fns, cur = [], None
    for ln in lines:
        if re.match(r"^(fn (cc|r)|oracle|action|perm_action|lproc) @", ln):
            cur = {"head": ln, "regs": None, "blocks": [], "lines": [ln]}
            m = re.match(r"^(?:fn (?:cc|r)|oracle|action|perm_action|lproc) @([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_.]*) (.*)$", ln)
            cur["kind"] = ln.split(" ")[0] if not ln.startswith("fn") else ln.split(" ")[1]
            cur["name"] = m.group(1)
            # five parenthesised lists
            cur["plists"] = re.findall(r"\(([^()]*)\)", m.group(2))
            fns.append(cur)
        elif ln.startswith("regs "):
            cur["regs"] = ln
            cur["lines"].append(ln)
        elif ln.startswith("^"):
            m = re.match(r"^\^([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_.]*)\((.*)\):$", ln)
            cur["blocks"].append({"name": m.group(1), "instrs": []})
            cur["lines"].append(ln)
        else:
            assert cur and cur["blocks"], f"instruction outside a block: {ln}"
            cur["blocks"][-1]["instrs"].append(ln)
            cur["lines"].append(ln)
    return fns


def parse_params(pl):
    out = []
    for p in [x for x in pl.split(", ") if x]:
        name, rest = p.split(":", 1)
        parts = rest.split(" ")
        out.append((name, parts[0], parts[1] if len(parts) > 1 else None))
    return out


def parse_regs(regs_line):
    m = re.match(r"^regs \{ (.*) \}", regs_line)
    body = m.group(1).strip()
    d = {}
    for p in [x for x in body.split(", ") if x]:
        n, t = p.split(":")
        d[n] = int(re.match(r"reg\[(\d+)\]", t).group(1))
    return d


def parse_instr(ln):
    lhs, rhs = (ln.split(" = ", 1) if " = " in ln else (None, ln))
    toks = re.findall(r"\([^()]*\)|\S+", rhs)
    op, ops = toks[0], toks[1:]
    return lhs, op, ops


# ----------------------------------------------------------------- hashing

def symbol_refs(fn_lines):
    """References in the body; the header line's own name is a definition."""
    return [m for m in re.findall(r"@([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_.]*)", "\n".join(fn_lines[1:]))]


def layout_leaves(layout):
    leaves = {}
    for k, v in layout.items():
        if k in ("globals", "interface_regions"):
            for n, e in v.items():
                if k == "globals":
                    leaves[f"globals.{n}"] = e
                else:
                    for r, ee in e.items():
                        leaves[f"interface_regions.{n}.{r}"] = ee
        else:
            leaves[k] = v
    return leaves


def closure_set(sym, fn, sections):
    """(data entries, layout leaves) in the symbol's closure set per §9.2."""
    layout = sections.get("layout", {})
    leaves = layout_leaves(layout)
    data = dict(l.split(" ") for l in sections.get("data", []))
    if sym["kind"] == "oracle":
        sel = {k: v for k, v in leaves.items()
               if not (k.startswith("interface_regions.") and not k.startswith(f"interface_regions.{sym['name']}."))}
        imgs = {k: data[k] for k in [v.get("image") for v in sel.values() if isinstance(v, dict)] if k}
        return imgs, sel
    if sym["kind"] == "lproc":
        names = re.findall(r"lift\.addr @([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_.]*)", "\n".join(fn["lines"]))
        sel = {f"globals.{n}": leaves[f"globals.{n}"] for n in names if f"globals.{n}" in leaves}
        imgs = {v["image"]: data[v["image"]] for v in sel.values()}
        return imgs, sel
    return {}, {}


def envelope(version, ptr_width, sig, body_lines, data, leaves, refs):
    parts = [f"isa={version}", f"ptr_width={ptr_width}", jcs(sig)] + body_lines
    parts += [f"data {n} {h}" for n, h in sorted(data.items())]
    parts += [f"layout {k} {jcs(v) if not isinstance(v, int) else v}" for k, v in sorted(leaves.items())]
    parts += list(refs)
    return ("\n".join(parts) + "\n").encode()


def compute_hashes(hdr, sections):
    symbols = {v["name"]: dict(v, index=k) for k, v in sections["symbols"].items()}
    fns = {f["name"]: f for f in parse_functions(sections.get("functions", []))}
    data_lines = dict(l.split(" ") for l in sections.get("data", []))
    names = list(symbols)
    # reference graph over symbols (interface-region names are not symbols)
    graph = {}
    for n in names:
        if n in fns:
            graph[n] = [r for r in symbol_refs(fns[n]["lines"]) if r in symbols]
        else:
            graph[n] = []
    # Tarjan SCC
    index, low, stack, on, sccs, counter = {}, {}, [], set(), [], [0]

    def strong(v):
        index[v] = low[v] = counter[0]; counter[0] += 1
        stack.append(v); on.add(v)
        for w in graph[v]:
            if w not in index:
                strong(w); low[v] = min(low[v], low[w])
            elif w in on:
                low[v] = min(low[v], index[w])
        if low[v] == index[v]:
            comp = []
            while True:
                w = stack.pop(); on.discard(w); comp.append(w)
                if w == v:
                    break
            sccs.append(sorted(comp))
    for v in names:

### 1.3 Deferred *(asserted non-semantic)*

Binary encodings (§16 text is the interchange form); mechanisation of
§14; device models beyond §7.3; the gate-name registry process; the
memory layout of the opaque C objects of §10–§11 (every constructor,
accessor and destructor is listed). **Minimality claims** (north star
§5) are not supported by cert 0.9 and MUST NOT appear in a certificate.
Decisions deferred to implementation, each with its default, are listed
in §17.

## 2. Terminology

- **Tier.** CC, R, Q. **Wire**, **bundle** `wire[N]` (little-endian).
- **Location** `reg[N]`: a named mutable cell of an oracle or lproc
  register file. **Pseudo-location**: an oracle interface input or
  classical argument, or an lproc classical parameter (§3.7), readable
  only in its declaring function, never written.
- **Region**: a Q function with a declared interface: **lifted oracle**
  (§5.2) or **quantum action** (§5.3); **permutation action** (§5.3).
- **lproc**: a lifted procedure with its own params and locations; it
  resolves names only to its own params and locations (§3.7).
- **Budget**: classical (B, heap_limit, stack_limit, depth_limit).
- **Frame**: per machine step: `old_pc`, slots (assignment, capture and
  delivery slots, §3.6), `flag`, `succ`.

def run_oracle(hdr, sections, name, ins, regions, B, depth_limit=64, stack_limit=None):
    fns = {f["name"]: f for f in parse_functions(sections["functions"])}
    oracle = fns[name]
    layout = sections.get("layout", {})
    if stack_limit is None:
        stack_limit = 2 ** layout.get("S_cells", 0)
    leaves = layout_leaves(layout)
    data = dict(l.split(" ") for l in sections.get("data", []))
    # memory + alloctab pre-entries: globals (layout order) then interface regions
    mem, alloctab = {}, []
    for gname, g in layout.get("globals", {}).items():
        img = bytes.fromhex(data[g["image"]])
        for i, b in enumerate(img):
            mem[g["base"] + i] = b
        alloctab.append([g["base"], g["size"], 1, 1])
    irs = layout.get("interface_regions", {}).get(name, {})
    mem_params = parse_params(oracle["plists"][3])
    for (rname, rtype, role) in mem_params:
        A, W = map(int, re.match(r"region\[(\d+),(\d+)\]", rtype).groups())
        base = irs[rname]["base"]
        for i, b in enumerate(regions[rname]):
            mem[base + i] = b
        alloctab.append([base, 2 ** A, 1, 1])
    addr_of = {g: e["base"] for g, e in layout.get("globals", {}).items()}
    addr_of.update({r: irs[r]["base"] for r in irs})
    ptr_mask = (1 << int(hdr["ptr_width"])) - 1
    state = {"steps": 0, "depth": 0, "result": 0, "sp": 0}

    def step():
        state["steps"] += 1
        if state["steps"] > B:
            raise Budget()

    def inrange(p, n):
        return any(l and b <= p and p + n <= b + sz for (b, sz, l, pin) in alloctab)

    def exec_fn(fn, env):
        regs = parse_regs(fn["regs"]) if fn["regs"] else {}
        widths = dict(regs)
        if fn["kind"] == "lproc":
            for (n, t, _) in parse_params(fn["plists"][0]) + parse_params(fn["plists"][1]):
                widths[n] = int(re.match(r"reg\[(\d+)\]", t).group(1))
        else:
            for (n, t, _) in parse_params(fn["plists"][0]):
                widths[n] = int(re.match(r"wire\[(\d+)\]", t).group(1))
        blocks = {b["name"]: b for b in fn["blocks"]}
        cur = fn["blocks"][0]["name"]
        pc = 0
        while True:
            ln = blocks[cur]["instrs"][pc]
            lhs, op, ops = parse_instr(ln)
            step()

            def rd(n):
                return env[n]

            def wr(n, v):
                env[n] = v & ((1 << widths[n]) - 1)
            if op.startswith("lift.const"):
                wr(lhs, int(ops[0]))
            elif op in ("lift.add", "lift.sub", "lift.mov"):
                a = rd(ops[0]); b = rd(ops[1]) if op != "lift.mov" else 0
                wr(lhs, a + b if op == "lift.add" else a - b if op == "lift.sub" else a)
            elif op.startswith("lift.icmp."):
                pred = op.split(".")[-1]; a, b = rd(ops[0]), rd(ops[1])
                wr(lhs, {"eq": a == b, "ne": a != b, "ult": a < b, "ule": a <= b}[pred])
            elif op.startswith("lift.zext."):
                wr(lhs, rd(ops[0]))
            elif op == "lift.addr":
                wr(lhs, addr_of[ops[0][1:]])
            elif op == "lift.boundscheck":
                if not inrange(rd(ops[0]), int(ops[1])):
                    raise Fault(FAULT["MEMFAULT"])
            elif op.startswith("lift.load."):
                n = int(op.split(".i")[1]) // 8; a = rd(ops[0])
                if not inrange(a, n):
                    raise Fault(FAULT["MEMFAULT"])
                wr(lhs, sum(mem.get(a + i, 0) << (8 * i) for i in range(n)))
            elif op == "lift.call":
                callee = fns[ops[0][1:]]
                args = [rd(x) for x in ops[1].strip("()").split(", ") if x]
                # §3.7 flag: depth or stack exhaustion; the stack is monotonic
                if state["depth"] + 1 > depth_limit or state["sp"] + frame_cells(callee) > stack_limit:
                    raise Fault(FAULT["DEPTH"])
                state["depth"] += 1
                state["sp"] += frame_cells(callee)
                cenv = {}
                for (pn, _, _), v in zip(parse_params(callee["plists"][0]), args):
                    cenv[pn] = v
                r = exec_fn(callee, cenv)
                wr(lhs, r)
            elif op == "lift.ret":
                if state["depth"] == 0:
                    raise Fault(FAULT["TRAP"])
                state["depth"] -= 1
                return rd(ops[0])
            elif op == "lift.retval":
                state["result"] ^= rd(ops[0])
                return None
            elif op == "qbranch":
                cur = ops[1][1:] if rd(ops[0]) else ops[2][1:]; pc = 0; continue
            elif op == "qjump":
                cur = ops[0][1:]; pc = 0; continue
            else:
                raise AssertionError(f"interpreter: unsupported row {op}")
            pc += 1

    env = {n: v for (n, _, _), v in zip(parse_params(oracle["plists"][0]), ins)}
    try:
        exec_fn(oracle, env)
        return (state["result"], 0, state["steps"])
    except Budget:
        return (0, FAULT["STEPS"], state["steps"])
    except Fault as f:
        return (0, f.code, state["steps"])

fn fail(message: impl std::fmt::Display) -> Error {
    Error::new(message.to_string())
}
fn uint(s: &str) -> Result<u128> {
    if let Some(hex) = s.strip_prefix("0x") {
        u128::from_str_radix(hex, 16).map_err(fail)
    } else {
        s.parse().map_err(fail)
    }
}
fn number(n: u128) -> Json {
    if n > 9_007_199_254_740_991 {
        Json::String(n.to_string())
    } else {
        Json::Number(n.to_string())
    }
}
fn vector_number(value: &BitVector) -> Json {
    match value.to_u128() {
        Ok(n) => number(n),
        Err(_) => Json::String(value.to_decimal()),
    }
}
fn vector_input(width: usize, raw: &str) -> Result<BitVector> {
    if let Some(hex) = raw.strip_prefix("0x") {
        if hex.is_empty() {
            return Err(fail("empty hexadecimal input"));
        }
        let mut value = BitVector::zero(width)?;
        for (i, c) in hex.chars().rev().enumerate() {
            let digit = c
                .to_digit(16)
                .ok_or_else(|| fail("invalid hexadecimal input"))?;
            for bit in 0..4 {
                if digit & (1 << bit) != 0 {
                    value.set(i * 4 + bit, true)?;
                }
            }
        }
        Ok(value)
    } else {
        BitVector::parse_unsigned_exact(width, raw)
    }
}
fn string(s: impl Into<String>) -> Json {
    Json::String(s.into())
}
fn object(pairs: impl IntoIterator<Item = (String, Json)>) -> Json {
    Json::Object(pairs.into_iter().collect())
}
fn budget(raw: &str, default: Budget) -> Result<Budget> {
    let v = raw
        .split(',')
        .map(|s| uint(s).map_err(|e| fail(format!("§5.4: invalid budget component {s:?}: {e}"))))
        .collect::<Result<Vec<_>>>()?;
    if v.len() != 1 && v.len() != 4 {
        return Err(fail("budget requires B or B,heap,stack,depth (§5.4)"));
    }
    let steps = u64::try_from(v[0]).map_err(|e| {
        Error::host_resource(format!(
            "§5.4: host limit: step budget exceeds the u64 counter: {e}"
        ))
    })?;
    Ok(if v.len() == 1 {
        Budget { steps, ..default }
    } else {
        Budget {
            steps,
            heap: v[1],
            stack: v[2],
            depth: v[3],
        }
    })
}
fn hex_buffer(len: usize) -> Result<String> {
    let count = len.checked_mul(2).ok_or_else(|| {
        Error::host_resource("§7.3/§10: host limit: CLI hex byte count exceeds usize".into())
    })?;
    let mut out = String::new();
    out.try_reserve_exact(count)
        .map_err(|e| Error::host_resource(format!("§7.3/§10: host limit: CLI hex bytes: {e}")))?;
    Ok(out)
}
fn hex(bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<String> {
    let mut out = hex_buffer(bytes.len())?;
    const DIGITS: &[u8; 16] = b"0123456789abcdef";
    for b in bytes {
        out.push(DIGITS[(b >> 4) as usize] as char);
        out.push(DIGITS[(b & 15) as usize] as char);
    }
    Ok(out)
}
#[test]
fn r21_cli_hex_storage_memory_limited() {
    if std::env::var_os("R21_HEX_CHILD").is_none() {
        let out = std::process::Command::new("python3").args(["-c",
            "import os,resource,sys; resource.setrlimit(resource.RLIMIT_AS,(256*1024*1024,)*2); resource.setrlimit(resource.RLIMIT_CORE,(0,0)); os.execv(sys.argv[1],sys.argv[1:])"])
            .arg(std::env::current_exe().unwrap())
            .args(["--exact", "r21_cli_hex_storage_memory_limited", "--nocapture"])
            .env("R21_HEX_CHILD", "1").output().unwrap();
        assert!(out.status.success(), "{out:?}");
        return;
    }
    std::panic::catch_unwind(|| {
        for len in [0, 1, 8, (1usize << 63) - 1, 1 << 63, usize::MAX] {
            let r = hex_buffer(len);
            if len <= 8 {
                assert!(r.unwrap().capacity() >= 2 * len);
            } else {
                let e = r.unwrap_err().to_string();
                assert!(e.contains('§') && e.contains("host limit"), "{e}");
            }
        }
        assert_eq!(hex(&[0, 127, 128, 255]).unwrap(), "007f80ff");
    })
    .expect("no CLI output length panic");
}
fn parse_hex(s: &str) -> Result<Vec<u8>> {
    if !s.len().is_multiple_of(2) || !s.bytes().all(|c| c.is_ascii_hexdigit()) {
        return Err(fail(
            "§16.1: invalid HEX (even-length hexadecimal expected)",
        ));
    }
    let mut out = Vec::new();
    out.try_reserve_exact(s.len() / 2)
        .map_err(|e| Error::host_resource(format!("§10: host limit: decoded reply bytes: {e}")))?;
    for i in (0..s.len()).step_by(2) {
        out.push(u8::from_str_radix(&s[i..i + 2], 16).map_err(fail)?);
    }
    Ok(out)
}
/// A binary64 as a canonical JSON number (RFC 8785 via `Json::parse`).
fn float_json(v: f64) -> Result<Json> {
    if !v.is_finite() {
        return Err(fail("§16.1: non-finite binary64 has no JSON number"));
    }
    Json::parse(&v.to_string())
}

                    ("steps".into(), number(b.steps as u128)),
                ]),
            ),
        ]),

            ("type".into(), string("qreg")),

# Checker resources — complete contexts and exact-rational display rules

Full revised proposal for council review, 2026-11-02; checker request 4
and `b-isa-spec-cwq`. Supersedes
`2026-11-02-contract-checker-resource-contexts.md`; proposal only.

## Defect classification and North Star scope

ISA 0.9 §12.1 enumerates complete invocation contexts, including default
coverage. §§11/12.3 permit a region under different arguments, budgets,
controls and schedule choices. Error entries already use common bounds
and explicit multiplicities. In contrast, flat region.resources and
gates_used maps do not choose an invocation or define a max/sum/common
bound. §8.2 defines event accounting but does not select the event list
for those maps. K12 binds an estimate's resources; it cannot choose the
meaning of another context's flat report.

A second gap predates this note: §12.3's resources {name:decimal} schema
has no encoding/comparison rule for non-terminating rational costs when
estimate is absent. §8.1 permits division, §8.2 aggregates rationals, and
§10.1 currently defines upward decimal encoding in the estimate contract.
The repair applies that encoding to every resources map, including old
single-context maps, while preserving exact accounting.

These are S0 contract gaps. North Star §§4.3–4.4/5.1 requires the complete
resource vector and substitutable scheduler/checker interfaces. Repeated,
controlled, default-covered and differently budgeted invocations stay
supported. Ignoring contexts, silently picking one or rejecting valid
rational costs cannot meet that scope. Unchecked reporting describes
verification status; it does not replace the repair.

## Alternatives and worked witnesses

| Choice | Consequence |
|---|---|
| Sum every child map | Misdefines peaks/makespan and double-counts nested leaves. |
| Common per-resource maximum | Could be a separately specified bound assertion; not exact event accounting. |
| Pick one context implicitly | Depends on traversal order and is not independently replayable. |
| Exact full-context records | Recommended; replay each event list and derive the root separately. |

Take `@flip(t)` with body `x t`. A root holds mutable t and in-role c
and executes these canonical instructions:

```text
action_call @flip (t) () () ()
qpred c
action_call @flip (t) () () ()
qunpred
```

At source points entry:0 and entry:2, each call contributes semantic
call_index=0 and instance=[], with no loop iterations. A native X entry
supports total controls 0..1, unit time, unit occupancy rate and sufficient
capacity in class fast. There are no other timed leaves; predicate-stack
instructions supply structural control. Its views are:

| Report | X native leaves | Time | Qubit-time | Peak |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| flip, forward, inherited controls 0 | 1 | 1 | 1 | 1 |
| flip, forward, inherited controls 1 | 1 | 1 | 2 | 2 |
| root, both interface wires live throughout | 2 | 2 | 4 | 2 |

The second child occupies root time [1,2) but reports its invocation-local
time 1. A zero-valued control still occupies its wire and scheduled gate
slot; these costs do not depend on a quantum predicate's basis value.
Summing child peaks gives 3 rather than 2; adding root and children
counts the same leaves twice. The second child includes its inherited
control in its footprint without transferring ownership from the caller.

Now set native duration to the legal expression {"op":"/","args":[1,3]}.
Child times are exactly 1/3; root time is 2/3. Child occupancies are 1/3
and 2/3; root occupancy is 4/3, with unchanged peaks. No finite decimal
can equal any of these thirds. Without estimate, exact decimal-vs-rational
equality would reject every legal time; the rule below instead compares
canonical displays of exact recomputed values.

As a borrow check, one wire lives over [0,2), borrowed during [0,1),
with owner rate 1 and dirty rate 1/4. Combined occupancy is 1/4+1=5/4,
not 9/4: borrower charges replace the owner's during that interval.

resource_contexts?:[{context_key,resources,gates_used,estimate?}]
```

is []. A standalone verified resource report binds one invocation and

### 11.1 Reference-library contract (K11)

**ISA-REF-LIB** (K11; b-isa-spec-8r4, from bennett-vm-47t).
This is the decided 0.9 contract. The catalog bound as ref version 0.9
is the file `spec/ref-0.9.json` whose four-line envelope hashes to
`5de4e4995350313e50217880ba1b95de39cd043283b726337d4894efb005d197`.
It publishes the complete 219-recipe catalog, byte-identical to the
accepted catalog snapshot. Consumers lacking the bound library report
`E_SCHEDULE_INFEASIBLE` as §11 requires. The witness below is not a
full-library hash.

**Serialization and identity.** The schema and family witnesses are
documented in `spec/ref-0.9-draft.README.md`. The JSON file is RFC 8785 canonical
UTF-8, with no trailing LF, containing exactly `isa`, `name`, `version`
and `recipes`. The first three are strings `0.9`, `ref`, `0.9`.
`recipes` is an array in bytewise `kind` order, then increasing parameter
tuple order. Each instantiated recipe has `kind`, its integer width or
other named integer parameters, `operations`, `scratch`, `removable`
and `source` (the motivating ISA sentence). A parameterised recipe
uses `parameters` (an ordered array of formals defined below) and `body` instead of the
instance's `operations`; its body uses the closed constructors below.
Recipes cover every §4 instruction/row, the §3.6 step, §3.7 activation
operations and §5.2 construction, with no implementation-private leaf.
Classical instructions/effects remain classical leaf events (§8.2),
not invented quantum gates. The README documents schema, parameters,
coverage and witnesses; it is not hashed and cannot alter operations.

The library hash is SHA-256 of this UTF-8 envelope (every line ends LF,
including the last); the fourth line is the **entire** canonical library
JSON, including all recipes, sources and versions, without an embedded
hash field:

```
isa=0.9
expansion=ref
version=0.9
<JCS library document>
```

`bindings.expansion` names `ref`, version `0.9`, and this 64-lowercase-hex
hash. This adopts §9.2's version-prefixed, LF-terminated **envelope
convention**, with explicit expansion-domain and library-version lines;
it is a new library rule, not §9.2's symbol signature/SCC envelope.
Motivation (0.8 §11): "a versioned document, identified in the schedule's
`bindings.expansion` as { name, version, hash }"; 0.8 §9.2: "Semantic
envelope ... every part ends with LF". A changed recipe, source, scratch
name or ordering changes the library hash; no schedule/certificate can
silently inherit that change (§12.1's realised-subject binding).

**Expansion and ordinals.** Recipe bodies are ordered arrays
of constructors: a gate (`gate`, ordered `wires`, optional `params`), an
allocation (`alloc`, `width`, `class`, `state`), release (`release`),
classical event (`classical`, `point`), counted loop (`for`, `from`,
`to`, `body`), named recipe expansion (`invoke`, ordered `args`), or

### 3.5 Predication

Every Q instruction with unitary semantics executes controlled on the
active predicate. `qpred w` pushes, `qunpred w` pops the same wire; the
stack is empty at region entry and exit. Allocation, borrowing, release,
region creation and image gates are **unconditional**; only gates
(including those inside copy/XOR/exchange/memory instructions) are
controlled.

### 3.6 The predicated step machine

**Index space.** `I[0..n)` = the **rows** (one index per instruction
line) of the oracle's blocks, blocks in text order and rows in text
order within each block; then the rows of every lproc in its **lproc
closure** (transitively reachable through `lift.call`, linked ones
included), placed as follows: partition the closure into its SCCs
(§9.2); a one-member SCC's **order key** is its member's closure hash, a
multi-member SCC's order key is its closure `scc_hash`; SCCs are placed
in increasing bytewise order of their keys (64-hex), the members of a
multi-member SCC consecutively in SCC index order; each lproc's binding
instances (§3.7) consecutively in binding-key order, each instance's rows
in block text order as for the oracle. The oracle and each lproc
instance therefore occupy a contiguous range of indices, and a **label
`^b` denotes the index of the first row of block b**. `HALT = n`; 2^P > n. Branch and call
targets are labels; `w = lift.label ^b` is the only way a location
obtains an index value; executors MAY re-index dispatch extensionally
**provided every label value observable through `lift.label` keeps its
canonical numeric value and a `qjumpr` to any in-range index reaches the
row that canonical index names**. **Terminators**: `qjump qbranch qjumpr lift.
ret lift.retval fault`; every block ends in one; no label names HALT;
**only `lift.retval` reaches HALT with success**.

**Row classes.** Each §4.2 row is an **assignment** (A: written
